## Phase 8 â€” Computer node

- [x] **T140** `clients/protocol/` â€” shared node protocol JSON schemas (commands, results, capabilities, auth)
  - Evidence: `clients/protocol/node-protocol.schema.json` (JSON Schema draft 2020-12) + `clients/protocol/README.md`. `$defs` for `NodeType`/`NodeStatus`/`ResultStatus`/`MessageType`/`Capability` (21 strings) and every message â€” `NodeRegistration`, `ServerHello`, `NodeHeartbeat`, `NodeHeartbeatAck`, `NodeCommand`, `NodeResult`, `NodeCapabilities`, `Goodbye` (which carries a required `node_id`). Every message is `additionalProperties: false`, unioned by a top-level `oneOf`. Normative and field-for-field with `app/agents/computer/protocol.py`. The README documents the transport and handshake, the two hard rules (outbound-only connect; a node credential is never a user), the message table, the "capabilities are advisory" note, structured-commands-only, and versioning. Contract tests in `server/tests/unit/test_node_protocol.py` (**38 passed**) assert schemaâ†”model agreement on required fields, properties, `additionalProperties`, and every enum, and validate both server-emitted and double-emitted frames against the schema.
- [x] **T141** `app/agents/computer/gateway.py` â€” node registration, heartbeat, command dispatch, result correlation
  - Evidence: `server/app/agents/computer/gateway.py` adds `NodeTransport` (Protocol), `ComputerNode` (`is_stale`/`to_capabilities`), and `NodeGateway(settings, authenticator, bus, command_timeout, max_nodes, require_auth)`. `register` returns a `ServerHello` and validates the credential + non-empty capability set; `heartbeat` refreshes liveness and emits `WINDOWS_ONLINE`; `deliver` correlates a `NodeResult` by `correlation_id` and returns `False` (never raises) for a stray; `dispatch` raises `NodeError` (`NODE_UNAVAILABLE`) for unknown/offline/no-capability/timeout/connection-drop/post-shutdown and `ToolError` when the node reported a failure; `disconnect` fails all pending commands; `sweep` marks stale nodes offline; queries are `get`/`maybe`/`list_nodes`/`find_by_capability(cap, online_only=True)`/`online_count`/`snapshot`; credentials via `issue_credential`/`revoke_credential`; `start`/`stop` + monitor loop. All bus payloads carry `node_id`. `require_auth=False` still authenticates the `node_id` against the registry â€” it merely skips the pre-issued token.
- [x] **T142** Node authentication (device tokens, mTLS-ready)
  - Evidence: `server/app/agents/computer/auth.py` â€” `hash_token` (sha256), `NodeCredential`, `NodeAuthenticator.issue/verify/revoke`. Tokens are generated, never supplied; hashed at rest; TTL-bounded; revocable; and mTLS-fingerprint-ready (the verifier accepts a caller-supplied fingerprint check). The credential authenticates the **node** only â€” there is no user principal anywhere on this path (Â§64.13). Tests cover issue/verify, expiry, revoke, and the `require_auth` matrix.
- [x] **T143** `app/tools/computer/` â€” screenshot, click, type, keys, window list, app control (structured commands only)

- [x] **T144** `app/agents/computer/agent.py` â€” ComputerAgent

It runs the Â§16 `ToolCallingLoop` with `self.agent_id` (so tool calls bind the ambient agent) and advertises the registry definitions. A run requires â‰¥1 advertised tool (refused at construction otherwise). `_available_methods` (explicit `methods` override â†’ gateway target capabilities â†’ `methods_for_capabilities`) and `_report` implement the Â§13 fallback path; the agent publishes ephemeral `TASK_PROGRESS` `plan`/`report`. Typed `UltronError` re-raises untouched; anything unexpected is wrapped in `AgentError`. `app/agents/computer/__init__.py` re-exports it.

## Phase 9 â€” Voice

- [x] **T150** `app/voice/stt/` â€” `STTEngine` ABC + faster-whisper adapter + availability probe
  - Evidence: `app/voice/stt/base.py` defines the seam: `AudioChunk` (`data`, `is_speech`, and the transport-supplied `end_of_utterance` added for client-side VAD / push-to-talk), `TranscriptChunk` (`text`, `is_final`, `utterance_id`, `language`, `duration`), and `STTEngine` (`name`, `sample_rate=16000`, `available`, `unavailable_reason`, `transcribe`, `transcribe_stream`, `aclose`). `NullSTTEngine` (`name="none"`) answers `available=False` and raises `DependencyUnavailableError("stt", â€¦)` from every call. Two adapters ship: the cloud `GroqWhisperSTT` (`stt/groq.py`, `name="groq"`) and the local `FasterWhisperSTT` (`stt/faster_whisper.py`). `stt/utterance.py` holds the Â§59.17 policy as pure, synchronous, independently tested types â€” `OverlapDeduper` (strips the duplicated head of a sliding window, bounded by `max_overlap`) and `UtteranceBoundary` (a run of silent chunks, not a single pause, ends a turn). `stt/availability.py` provides `faster_whisper_available` / `faster_whisper_unavailable_reason`. `stt/__init__.py` `create_stt_engine` builds the configured engine and, when `STT_FALLBACK_ENGINE` names a second, chains it through `FallbackSTTEngine`; an unknown/`none` selection or a disabled subsystem yields the honest null engine rather than a fake. PCM handling is correct in both adapters: `GroqWhisperSTT._wrap_pcm` frames raw PCM as a RIFF/WAVE container before upload (the HTTP endpoint needs a header), and `FasterWhisperSTT._decode_audio` converts int16 PCM to float32 via a lazily imported `numpy`, raising `DependencyUnavailableError` when it is absent.
- [x] **T151** `app/voice/tts/` â€” `TTSEngine` ABC + Piper adapter + availability probe
  - Evidence: `app/voice/tts/base.py` defines `TTSEngine` (with `content_type: str = "audio/L16"` so a caller knows the downlink encoding) and the honest `NullTTSEngine`. Adapters: cloud `ElevenLabsTTS` (`tts/elevenlabs.py`, default output `pcm_22050`) and `EdgeTTSTTS` (`tts/edge_tts.py`, 24 kHz `audio/mpeg`), plus local `PiperTTS` (`tts/piper.py`). `tts/segments.py` `split_sentences` lets synthesis start on the first sentence instead of the whole reply. `tts/availability.py` provides `piper_available`/`piper_unavailable_reason` and `edge_tts_available`/`edge_tts_unavailable_reason`. `tts/__init__.py` `create_tts_engine` mirrors the STT factory, including the `FallbackTTSEngine` chain and the null-engine degrade. No engine is assumed present: a selected engine whose binary or key is missing stays a real object with `available=False` that raises a clear 503 on use (Â§59.18, Â§64.11).
- [x] **T152** `app/voice/wake/` â€” wake-word event handling from ESP32 â†’ `WAKE_DETECTED`
  - Evidence: `app/voice/wake/detector.py` `WakeWordDetector` matches configured wake words with a **word-boundary** regex (case-insensitive, so `"ultron"` matches `"Ultron, playâ€¦"` but never `"ultronix"`), returns a `WakeMatch(word, command)` with the wake word stripped from the command text, and publishes `WAKE_DETECTED` from two entry points: `detect()` for a server-side transcript (`source="stt"`) and `signal()` for a device-side detection (Â§26 ESP32, `source="esp32"`). Both funnel to one event so the manager is the single place that reacts to a wake. `build_wake_detector` reads `settings.wake_words` with an override. `wake/__init__.py` re-exports it.
- [x] **T153** `app/voice/manager.py` â€” voice sessions, STTâ†’Coreâ†’Agentâ†’TTS pipeline
  - Evidence: `app/voice/manager.py` `VoiceManager` owns sessions (`create_session`, `get`, `list_sessions`, `end_session`, `sweep` for the idle TTL) and the Â§25 pipeline via `handle_audio` (streams `AudioChunk` â†’ `STTEngine.transcribe_stream` â†’ one `run_turn` per final utterance) and `run_turn` (think â†’ speak). The model/agent step is an **injected** `Responder` callable, so the manager imports no provider and is tested with a fake; it drives the agent through the shared `ToolCallingLoop` (client `"voice"`) built by `app/voice/responder.py` `build_agent_responder` â€” the same loop the chat routes use (Â§59.21, voice is another front door, not a parallel stack), opening one `session_scope` per turn. `VoiceState` (`listening`/`thinking`/`speaking`/`ended`), `VoiceTurn`, `VoiceReply`, and `build_voice_manager` complete the module; `app/voice/fallback.py` holds `FallbackSTTEngine`/`FallbackTTSEngine`.
- [x] **T154** Streaming/partial transcripts + interruption (barge-in) handling
  - Evidence: partial results and turn boundaries publish `STT_PARTIAL`/`STT_FINAL` (Â§59.17), and each turn publishes `VOICE_LISTENING`/`VOICE_THINKING`/`VOICE_SPEAKING`, `TTS_STARTED` (the answer text) and `TTS_COMPLETED` (`interrupted`). `VoiceManager.interrupt()` cancels the in-flight task, and the newly added `barge_in()` raises the interrupt flag **without** cancelling the consuming loop â€” the long-lived stream keeps listening after the user talks over ULTRON while the audio loop stops delivering at the next chunk. An interrupted assistant turn is *marked* (`VoiceTurn.interrupted`), not dropped. `handle_audio(..., on_audio=â€¦)` streams each synthesized chunk to a transport. `app/api/routes/voice.py` exposes `GET /voice/engines` (the honest availability report, Â§64.11) and `WS /voice/ws` (raw audio up; JSON `session`/`state`/`stt_partial`/`stt_final`/`answer`/`tts_completed`/`ended`/`error` and binary audio down; control frames `interrupt`, `utterance_end`, `stop`; token via `?token=`). The container wires STT/TTS/responder/bus and `ContainerProtocol.voice` exposes it.
- [x] **T155** Phase 9 tests â€” session lifecycle, partial events, interruption; adapters skipped when binaries absent
  - Evidence: **96 Phase-9 tests.** `test_voice_stt.py` (33): engine/base/store contracts, `OverlapDeduper`/`UtteranceBoundary` policy, availability probes, factory/fallback selection, `end_of_utterance` close, and `_wrap_pcm` WAV framing. `test_voice_tts.py` (33): interfaces, `split_sentences`, factory/fallback, availability. `test_voice_wake.py` (9): word-boundary matching, wake-word stripping, `detect` vs `signal` sources, `WAKE_DETECTED` payloads. `test_voice_manager.py` (13): session lifecycle, partial/final events, `run_turn`, `on_audio` streaming, `interrupt`, `barge_in`. `test_voice_routes.py` (5): `GET /voice/engines`, WS session/audio/answer flow, barge-in, and `utterance_end` closing a turn. `test_voice_container.py` (3): the container builds a fully wired manager (engines, shared bus, responder), memoises it, and yields null engines when voice is disabled. Adapter tests skip/gate on binaries and keys being absent rather than asserting a fake install (Â§64.11).
- [x] **T156** **PHASE 9 verification**
  - Evidence: `ruff check app tests` â†’ **clean**; `mypy app tests` â†’ **clean (285 source files)**; `pytest tests/unit tests/integration -q` â†’ **2380 passed, 7 skipped**. The UI voice client (`UIPROTO/src/lib/voice.ts`) and its mic-button wiring were `tsc`-clean and built into `server/app/static/`.

**Phase 9 gate:** missing STT/TTS engines degrade honestly, never fake â€” **met.** A disabled or unconfigured subsystem returns `NullSTTEngine`/`NullTTSEngine` (`available=False`) whose calls raise `DependencyUnavailableError` (503), the `/voice/engines` route reports that reason verbatim, and the client hides/states unavailability rather than offering a button that fabricates a transcript or silence.

## Phase 10 â€” ESP32 / IoT

- [x] **T160** `app/devices/protocol.py` â€” device message envelope, device fields per spec Â§26
  - Evidence: `app/devices/protocol.py` defines the device wire contract: `DeviceMessageType` (`register`/`registered`/`heartbeat`/`heartbeat_ack`/`event`/`command`/`result`/`goodbye`), `DeviceEventType` with the six Â§26 events verbatim (`wake_word`, `button_press`, `sensor_event`, `audio_stream`, `device_online`, `device_offline`), and the models `DeviceRegister`/`DeviceRegistered`/`DeviceHeartbeat`/`DeviceHeartbeatAck`/`DeviceEventMessage`/`DeviceCommand`/`DeviceResult`/`DeviceGoodbye`/`DeviceCapabilities` (all `extra="forbid"`). The envelope carries the Â§26 device fields (`device_id`, `name`, `device_type`, `capabilities`, `status`, `last_seen`, `firmware_version`) and **no board is named**: `device_type` is a free string and capabilities are advertised, not enumerated, so a new board needs no Core change (Â§26). `parse_message` is the transport's single validated entry point (unknown/missing type, missing field, unknown field and bad enum all raise `InvalidInputError`, never a crash). `core_event_for` maps `wake_word`â†’`WAKE_DETECTED`, `device_online`â†’`DEVICE_CONNECTED`, `device_offline`â†’`DEVICE_DISCONNECTED` case-insensitively; the three application events have no canonical core event and return `None`. The wire status reuses the registry's `DeviceStatus` so the two cannot drift (D021). Tests: `tests/unit/test_device_protocol.py` (21).
- [x] **T161** `app/devices/manager.py` â€” registry, status, `last_seen`, capabilities, auth
  - Evidence: `app/devices/manager.py` implements `DeviceManager` â€” the transport-agnostic server half of the Â§26 device protocol, mirroring the T141 `NodeGateway` shape. **Registry:** `register` accepts/replaces a `DeviceConnection` (device fields Â§26 plus live counters), refuses with `accepted=False` (the protocol's refusal channel) when a credential is missing/invalid under `require_auth` or when `max_connections` is reached, and closes the superseded transport on a reconnect. **Liveness:** `heartbeat` refreshes `last_seen`, `status`, `capabilities` and `firmware_version` (unknown device â†’ typed `NotFoundError`, so a device always registers first); `sweep(now=â€¦)` marks devices past `heartbeat_timeout` offline deterministically (no sleeps) and the `start`/`stop` monitor drives it on an interval. **Events:** `report` records a `DeviceEventMessage` and publishes the canonical counterpart via `core_event_for` â€” `wake_word`â†’`WAKE_DETECTED` (Â§25), `device_online`/`device_offline`â†’`DEVICE_CONNECTED`/`DEVICE_DISCONNECTED` (Â§19) â€” returning whether a canonical event fired (`button_press`/`sensor_event`/`audio_stream` return `False` and are left to the IoT agent, T164); `device_offline` also moves status. **Command/result:** `dispatch` sends a structured `DeviceCommand` (never a shell string, Â§13) with a `command_id` and awaits the correlated `DeviceResult` via `deliver`; a dispatch to an unknown/offline device or one missing the required capability raises typed `DeviceError`; a device-reported failure raises `ToolError`; a timeout or dropped connection raises `DeviceError`; a stray/late result is logged and dropped, never fatal. **Auth:** the device credential reuses the shared node identity model (`NodeAuthenticator`/`hash_token`, Â§30/Â§64.13) via `issue_credential`/`revoke_credential`, deliberately the same principal split D021/T332 unify (`devices.auth_token_hash`). No permission decision here â€” Â§15's gate is the pipeline's; the principal only names the audit actor. Tests: `tests/unit/test_device_manager.py` (42) over the new `tests/support/device_double.py` (`FakeDevice`/`FakeDeviceTransport`, JSON on the wire).
- [x] **T162** `app/devices/esp32/` â€” JSON-over-WebSocket transport, command/response, keepalive
  - Evidence: `app/devices/esp32/transport.py` is the reference device connection (spec Â§26/Â§64.9) and is **board-agnostic** â€” a generic JSON-over-WebSocket session, named for the reference board only because that is the firmware the T160 protocol was drawn against (T167's gate). `WebSocketDeviceTransport` is the manager-facing `DeviceTransport` (`send`/`close`, the latter tolerating an already-closed socket) so the registry never sees a socket; `run_device_session` runs the whole handshake: the first frame must be a `register` (a malformed or non-register opening frame closes rather than half-serves), the manager answers `registered` (accepted **or refused** â€” a device credential authenticates a device, never a user, Â§30/Â§64.13), then the loop reads `heartbeat`â†’`heartbeat_ack`, `event`â†’`report`, `result`â†’`deliver`, `goodbye`â†’clean end. A malformed frame mid-session is logged and skipped, never dropping the socket (T160's contract); every exit path disconnects the device in a `finally` so capabilities stop being offered (Â§64.10). **Keepalive** = the device heartbeat + the manager's `sweep`; the socket layer owns ping/pong. The reader is written against a tiny `DeviceSocket` protocol, so it is unit-testable with a queue double and no network. `app/api/routes/devices.py` is the thin mount: `GET /devices` / `GET /devices/{id}` (authenticated, `404`/`NOT_FOUND`) and `WS /devices/ws` (refuses with `1008` when `DEVICES_ENABLED=false`); the container builds a lazy `device_manager` (`DeviceSettings`, shared event bus) and starts/stops its monitor with `DEVICES_ENABLED`, and `ContainerProtocol` declares it (`app/container.py`, `app/api/dependencies.py`, `app/main.py`). Tests: `tests/unit/test_device_transport.py` (9) and `tests/unit/test_devices_routes.py` (8) over `tests/support/device_double.py` (`FakeDeviceSocket`).
- [x] **T163** Optional MQTT transport adapter
  - Evidence: `app/devices/mqtt/` is the second way a device reaches the Core (Â§26), behind the `iot` extra (`paho-mqtt>=2.1`). The broker is a **seam, not a dependency**: `transport.py` defines the tiny `MqttClient` protocol (`publish`/`messages`) and two broker-agnostic pieces â€” `MqttDeviceTransport`, the manager-facing `DeviceTransport` that publishes each outbound frame as JSON to `{prefix}/devices/{id}/down` (its `close` is a no-op: MQTT has no per-device socket to cut, a device goes offline by heartbeat expiry), and `MqttDeviceBridge`, which subscribes once to `{prefix}/devices/+/up` and routes `register`â†’`register`, `heartbeat`â†’`heartbeat_ack`, `event`â†’`report`, `result`â†’`deliver`, `goodbye`â†’`disconnect` into the **same** `DeviceManager` the WebSocket session uses, keyed by the device id in the topic and creating the per-device down-topic transport on demand. A malformed or off-topic message is logged and skipped, never killing the bridge (T160's contract), and `device_id_from_topic` rejects a foreign prefix/shape. `availability.py` probes `paho` (cached, with a reason) and `client.py` is the concrete `PahoMqttClient` â€” a `paho-mqtt` client whose threaded callback loop is bridged to asyncio via a thread-safe `asyncio.Queue` (`loop.call_soon_threadsafe`) and `asyncio.to_thread` for the blocking `connect`/`publish`/`disconnect` â€” imported lazily so ULTRON runs without the extra. `build_mqtt_client` returns `None` (not a crash) when the extra is absent, and the container starts the bridge only when `MQTT_ENABLED` is on, logging the misconfiguration and falling back to the WebSocket transport otherwise (Â§64.11). The paho client swaps out behind `MqttClient` (e.g. for `aiomqtt`) with no change above it. Tests: `tests/unit/test_device_mqtt.py` (22) over `tests/support/device_double.py` (`FakeMqttClient`, finite-frame and queued modes) â€” no broker, no extra.
- [x] **T164** `app/agents/iot/agent.py` â€” IoT agent
  - Evidence: `app/agents/iot/agent.py` is the worker for Â§26's device layer â€” a declaration plus one `run`, shaped like `ComputerAgent` and handed its collaborators (the `DeviceManager`, optionally a `ToolCallingLoop`). It handles the events T161 deliberately leaves to it: `DeviceManager.report` publishes the canonical counterpart of `wake_word`/`device_online`/`device_offline` and returns `True`, while `button_press`/`sensor_event`/`audio_stream` return `False` and are the agent's to act on â€” `handle_event(event)` is that entry point and runs the same body as `run`. Two paths, one report: a **deterministic** one dispatches a structured command (`device_id`+`action`+`arguments`) straight through `DeviceManager.dispatch` (Â§13 â€” a structured `DeviceCommand`, never a shell string) so a deployment with no device tool family still works, and an **optional model** one drives `ToolCallingLoop` (Â§16) pre-advertised with `device.*` tool definitions, refusing at construction to advertise tools without both a router and an executor. Every run publishes a `TASK_PROGRESS` (`phase="plan"`, `persist=False`, Â§19) and returns a report carrying the resolved device view (`to_capabilities`), the dispatch result, the model answer/stop-reason when a loop ran, and the manager `snapshot`. It never holds a socket and never imports a board (Â§26: no board hard-coded in Core), a missing device action target is `InvalidInputError` (422) and an unknown device is the manager's typed `DeviceError` (503); `UltronError` is re-raised and anything else becomes `AgentError`. Tests: `tests/unit/test_iot_agent.py` (11) over the real manager + `FakeDevice` and a `FakeProvider`-driven loop.
- [x] **T165** Device state persistence + `device_events`
  - Evidence: `app/devices/store.py` makes the live device view durable through a **seam** â€” the `DeviceStore` protocol (`save_device`/`mark_offline`/`record_event`) is declared in `manager.py` beside `DeviceTransport`, so the `DeviceManager` persists without importing SQLAlchemy and stays testable with `NullDeviceStore` (the honest no-op default, Â§64.11). The manager now calls the store on registration, heartbeat, the transition offline (`disconnect`/`sweep`/a `device_offline` event) and every reported event; every call goes through `DeviceManager._persist`, which logs `device.store_failed` and continues so a database outage never drops a device or fails a heartbeat. `SqlDeviceStore` (`app/devices/store.py`) writes each call as one `session_scope` unit of work: a read-then-upsert of the `devices` row and an append to `device_events` through `DeviceRepository`/`DeviceEventRepository`. **The wireâ†”row join:** Â§26's free-string `device_id` is stored under `metadata["device_id"]` and resolved by the new portable `DeviceRepository.find_by_external_id` (`json_extract` on SQLite, `->>` on PostgreSQL, via SQLAlchemy's JSON index type) â€” forward-compatible with T332's first-class `node_id`. A `mark_offline` leaves a `DISABLED` row disabled (a write never undoes a revocation, the same rule as the repository's `heartbeat`); an event for an unknown device is dropped with a debug diagnostic, not a crash. The container builds a lazy `device_store` from the shared `session_scope` and hands it to the manager only when `DEVICES_ENABLED` is on, so a disabled subsystem never opens a session (`app/container.py`). Tests: `tests/unit/test_device_store.py` (16) â€” in-memory SQLite with the real schema, store-in-isolation and manager-wired, including a store that always raises.
- [x] **T166** Phase 10 tests â€” device lifecycle, event flow, auth, wake-word path
  - Evidence: **142 Phase-10 tests.** Per-layer: `test_device_protocol.py` (21) wire contract and canonical mapping; `test_device_manager.py` (42) registry, liveness, dispatch/correlation, typed unavailability; `test_device_transport.py` (9) the JSON-over-WebSocket session; `test_devices_routes.py` (8) `GET /devices` and `WS /devices/ws`; `test_device_mqtt.py` (22) the broker seam; `test_iot_agent.py` (11) the IoT worker; `test_device_store.py` (16) persistence. The new `test_device_phase10.py` (13) tests the **phase at its seams**, with a real WS session over `FakeDeviceSocket`, a real `SqlDeviceStore` on in-memory SQLite and a recording bus: lifecycle (`register` â†’ `heartbeat` â†’ `wake_word` â†’ `goodbye`, and a reconnect that reuses one record and one row, and a sweep that retains the offline device), event flow (connect precedes device events; a canonical versus an application event; every event persisted in order; the envelope naming the device), the auth boundary (credential required, an issued one admitted and persisted, a revoked or bad one refused without naming the device), and the wake path (a device wake and a detector wake are the same `WAKE_DETECTED`; an unknown device is a typed `NotFoundError`).
- [x] **T167** **PHASE 10 verification**
  - Evidence: **done.** `ruff check app tests` â†’ **clean**; `mypy app tests` â†’ **clean (304 source files)**; `pytest tests/unit tests/integration` â†’ **2522 passed, 7 skipped in 3:05** (2 skips = symlinks unsupported on this platform; 5 = the `requires_playwright` module). The phase scope (Â§26 device layer, plus Â§64.9/Â§64.15) is delivered and tested: the wire contract `app/devices/protocol.py` (T160), the registry/liveness/dispatch `app/devices/manager.py` (T161), the reference JSON-over-WebSocket session `app/devices/esp32/` and the `GET /devices` + `WS /devices/ws` mount (T162), the optional MQTT seam `app/devices/mqtt/` (T163), the `IoTAgent` `app/agents/iot/agent.py` (T164), persistence `app/devices/store.py` + the `DeviceStore` seam (T165) and the phase suites (T166) â€” **142 Phase-10 tests** across `test_device_protocol` (21), `test_device_manager` (42), `test_device_transport` (9), `test_devices_routes` (8), `test_device_mqtt` (22), `test_iot_agent` (11), `test_device_store` (16) and `test_device_phase10` (13). **The gate ("no specific ESP32 board hard-coded in Core") is closed**: a scan of `app/` finds no board *model* anywhere â€” a device is a free-form `device_type` string plus advisory capabilities, `NodeType.ESP32` is a *kind* (not a board) and `ESP32_ONLINE`/`ESP32_OFFLINE` are the Â§64.15 event names the spec fixes, so `DeviceManager`, `protocol.py` and `IoTAgent` never branch on a device type and a new board is a new row, not a Core change. **Server half** (per Â§61 cannot be run here, tracked in `todo.md` B1): a live ESP32 over `WS /devices/ws` â€” no board is attached here â€” and the live `PahoMqttClient` broker path â€” the `iot` extra is not installed and no broker is present, so the paho client is exercised only behind the `MqttClient` seam with `FakeMqttClient`. Both must be run on the server before the project-wide Â§56 definition of done is claimed for the phase's live path.

**Phase 10 gate:** no specific ESP32 board hard-coded in Core

## Definition of Done (spec Â§56) â€” live status

Verified on this Windows machine: `import app.main` succeeds, the full unit
suite passes (`2594 passed, 2 skipped`), and each item's behaviour is exercised
by a passing unit test. Items that require a live server, Docker, or a real model
host are marked **unverified here** with the reason â€” per the truthfulness rule,
nothing is marked done without evidence.

| # | Check | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Server starts | done (import + factory) | `import app.main` OK; app built by `create_app()`; a live `uvicorn` start is a machine-restricted action (see note 1) |
| 2 | PostgreSQL connects | unverified here | No Docker/Postgres on this machine; engine + migrations built (T026); exercised on Ubuntu |
| 3 | Redis connects | unverified here | No Docker/Redis here; Redis module + tests pass (`test_redis_*`); exercised on Ubuntu |
| 4 | API works | done (tests) | Routers mounted (`main._include_routers`); route tests pass (`test_event_stream_routes`, `test_health_*`) |
| 5 | WebSocket works | done (tests) | `/nodes/ws`, `/devices/ws`, `/voice/ws` sessions tested (`test_*_transport`, device/voice tests) |
| 6 | Health endpoint works | done (tests) | `/health`, `/ready`, `/metrics` implemented and tested |
| 7 | Agent Manager works | done (tests) | `test_agent_manager.py`, `test_agent_registry.py`, `test_agent_loop.py` pass |
| 8 | Multiple agents run concurrently | done (tests) | Concurrency covered by agent-manager/loop tests |
| 9 | Task persistence works | done (tests) | `test_task_manager.py`, `test_task_state.py`, `test_task_graph.py` pass; DB layer present |
| 10 | Event bus works | done (tests) | `test_event_bus.py`, `test_event_handlers.py`, `test_event_types.py` pass |
| 11 | Permissions work | done (tests) | `test_permissions.py` passes; six-level gate + audit |
| 12 | Tool registry works | done (tests) | `test_tool_registry.py`, `test_tool_executor.py`, `test_tool_runtime.py` pass |
| 13 | Ollama integration works | unverified here | Adapter + tests exist; a real Ollama run needs a model host (Ubuntu/production) |
| 14 | Model Router works | done (tests) | `test_router.py` passes (provider adapters + capability routing) |
| 15 | Coding Agent works | done (tests) | `test_coding_agent.py`, `test_coding_verify.py` pass |
| 16 | OpenCode integration works | unverified here | Integration shell/tooling present but needs an external OpenCode host to run end-to-end |
| 17 | Browser Agent works | done (unit) / unverified E2E | `test_browser_agent.py`, `test_browser_session.py`, `test_browser_tools.py` pass; real Playwright run is `tests/integration/test_browser_playwright.py` (needs a browser) |

**Notes**

1. Starting a live server is prohibited on this machine by policy, so items 1â€“3
   are confirmed by import, factory construction and passing tests rather than by
   a running socket. The server is expected to be exercised on Ubuntu.
2. "unverified here" means no evidence could be produced on this machine. These
   are not failures; they are items whose verification requires an environment
   (Docker, a model host, a browser, a live socket) that is unavailable here.
3. Unit tests are the evidence for behavioural items; integration tests are
   skipped cleanly when their dependencies are absent (`tests/integration/`).

## Decision log

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D001 | Dev stack = Docker Desktop with `postgres`, `redis`, `ultron-api` only | 8 GB laptop; Ollama/Playwright/multi-agent reserved for production or on-demand |
| D002 | No local model during development; cloud providers for real calls | Avoids large model downloads on the laptop; Ollama is a production/host concern |
| D003 | Full monorepo skeleton in Phase 0 | Matches spec Â§4; clients/ and deployment/ are first-class, not afterthoughts |
| D004 | Hand-rolled DI container in `app/container.py` | Spec Â§57: prefer boring infrastructure over clever abstractions; avoids a DI framework dependency |
| D005 | Extra package `app/workspaces/` beyond spec Â§4 tree | Required by spec Â§46; Â§4's tree is structural, not exhaustive |
| D005a | Notifications live in `app/tools/notifications/`, not `app/notifications/` | Notification delivery is a tool invoked through the tool pipeline (spec Â§45 with Â§16), so it belongs with its sibling tools rather than in a top-level package |
| D006 | `clients/protocol/` holds shared node protocol schemas | Single source of truth for the Windows node â†” server contract (spec Â§13, Â§28, Â§42) |
| D007 | `server/pyproject.toml` per spec Â§4; tool config lives there | Keeps root free of Python packaging concerns |
| D008 | One package added to Â§4 tree marked as a deviation | See D005; recorded so the deviation is intentional and traceable |
| D009 | Integration tests skip cleanly when PostgreSQL/Redis/Docker absent | Test suite must pass on a bare checkout without paid APIs or services |
| D010 | Task DAG hand-rolled instead of `networkx` | Avoids a heavyweight dependency for a small, well-understood algorithm |

#### T017 delivery notes

Scope is the four uses the task line names â€” cache, locks, pub/sub, transient
state. Spec Â§24 lists six, and the other three arrive with their consumers rather
than landing here unasked: queues with the event bus (T031), rate limiting with
the security work, and event-stream coordination (T023, since built as SSE).
All three are built on the primitives in this module.

Four decisions were confirmed before implementation, because the spec is silent on
each and every one is expensive to reverse later:

- **Locks are token + compare-and-delete.** `SET NX PX` then `DEL` is wrong: a
  holder that stalls past its lease frees the *next* holder's lock. Release and
  extend are both Lua compare-and-act. No implicit renewal â€” a background renewal
  task outlives the work that wanted the lock, so `extend` is the caller's call.
- **A cache read raises by default.** `get_json` raises `RedisError`;
  `get_or_none` is the separate, named cache-aside path that treats an outage as a
  miss and logs it. Collapsing the two would turn a Redis outage into a silent
  cold cache.
- **Values are JSON, JSON-native or nothing.** A `datetime`, `UUID`, `Decimal`, or
  `set` is refused at the write rather than coerced via `default=str`, because a
  coerced value reads back as a string with no way to tell it was ever a datetime.
  `tuple` â†’ `list` is the one silent change, and it is pinned by a test.
- **Keys and channels are namespaced under `ultron:`**, in separate segments, so a
  cache key and a pub/sub channel of the same logical name cannot collide and a
  shared instance can be swept safely.

Two notes on the delivery:

- `LockUnavailableError` was added to `app/core/errors.py`. It reuses
  `ErrorCode.CONFLICT` rather than adding a code â€” to a client, "someone else got
  there first" is the same answer â€” but is a distinct type, and is deliberately
  **not** retryable: a blanket "retry on `RedisError`" is right for an outage and
  a thundering herd for contention.
- The dev dependency is now `fakeredis[lua]`, not `fakeredis`. The `lua` extra is
  required, not optional: without it `fakeredis` answers `EVAL` with "unknown
  command", leaving the lock's safety-critical path as the one thing in the module
  the unit suite cannot exercise. A test asserts the capability is present so the
  failure names the missing dependency instead of surfacing inside whichever lock
  test runs first.

Unit coverage is 86 tests. The lock tests deliberately let a lease expire
underneath its holder and then assert the loser's release leaves the winner's lock
intact â€” the failure mode a "acquire, release, key gone" test cannot see. Reverting
the compare-and-delete to a bare `DEL` was confirmed to fail that test.

`LockUnavailableError` and `RedisError` are now sampled by
`tests/unit/test_core_errors.py` as well, so the T012 contract suite covers the
retry/status/secret rules the two share. A cached-value detail is the reason: the
retryable-code agreement test and the HTTP-status table are module-wide, so a new
error class is checked automatically for *self-consistency* but nothing else had
asserted the verdict that actually matters here â€” that a Redis outage is retryable
while lock contention is not, and that the contention error is not a `RedisError`
subclass that a blanket "retry on `RedisError`" would swallow.

#### T018 delivery notes

All six checks from the task line are present, but only four can be real today:
`agent_runtime` (T044) and `event_bus` (T031) have no implementation to probe. They
are registered as absent and reported as `skipped` rather than dropped, because a
check that silently vanishes from the report is indistinguishable from one that
passed. The report always carries the six names, so `/ready` can be diffed against
a known list; T019 registers the four real ones at startup and the last two appear
on their own when their subsystems land, with no edit here.

Decisions worth writing down, because each is a judgement the spec does not make:

- **Only PostgreSQL gates readiness.** Redis, Ollama and the filesystem are
  optional and degrade. The authority chain already says this (PostgreSQL is the
  store; Redis is cache, locks and pub/sub), and a readiness gate that restarts a
  container because the cache is down trades a degraded service for no service.
- **Liveness never touches a dependency.** `/health` answers "this process is
  alive" and nothing else. A liveness probe that consulted PostgreSQL would restart
  every replica during a database blip, turning one dependency's outage into an
  outage of the whole fleet. That is why the two endpoints are separate methods
  rather than one probe with a flag.
- **The probes raise; the wrapper classifies.** `check_postgresql`, `check_redis`
  and `check_ollama` let `DatabaseError`, `RedisError` and
  `LocalModelUnavailableError` propagate instead of catching them into a status.
  Those types carry a retry verdict and an HTTP status that a health check would
  flatten away, and a caller using a check directly would otherwise get a
  healthy-looking result from a failed database. Status assignment is the
  wrapper's job, where required-vs-optional is known.
- **`TimeoutError` is caught separately.** It is not a subclass of `OSError`, so
  the `except OSError` net that turns an unreachable host into `failed` does not
  see it. A silently unbounded probe is the failure mode that takes the whole
  `/ready` endpoint down with it, so the budget is enforced per check and a
  timed-out optional check degrades rather than failing.
- **The filesystem check writes a real file.** `os.access(W_OK)` returns `True`
  on paths that a read-only mount or a restrictive ACL still refuses, so it would
  report a filesystem as writable that cannot accept the uploads the API takes.
  The probe creates and deletes a temp file instead, and its leftovers are the
  bug it was written to prevent.

On the hosted Ollama: `OLLAMA_URL` was already configurable and the default stays
`http://localhost:11434`. What was missing is that a hosted instance needs a
credential, so `OLLAMA_API_KEY` was added (as a `SecretStr`, alongside the existing
provider keys) and `OllamaSettings.auth_headers()` omits the header entirely when
no key is set rather than sending an empty one. Local and hosted are then the same
code path â€” `GET /api/tags` with an optional bearer â€” instead of two code paths
that drift. No inference code was touched: T018 only answers whether the provider
is reachable.

Unit coverage is 55 tests, and the suite injects every dependency (fake engine,
`Redis` double, fake HTTP client, `tmp_path`) so it runs on a bare checkout with
no PostgreSQL, no Redis and no network. Four mutations were applied and reverted to
confirm the tests actually bite, since a health report that is wrong in the
permissive direction is worse than no report:

| Mutation | Caught by |
|---|---|
| required set emptied, so PostgreSQL becomes optional | 6 tests, incl. a timed-out required check going ready |
| expected checks omitted instead of reported `skipped` | 5 tests |
| per-run lock removed | the concurrent-callers test (one run, many waiters) |
| `asyncio.timeout` removed | the hung-check test, which then took 60s to fail |

The last two are the ones a happy-path suite usually misses, and the lock in
particular is the difference between one probe per TTL window and a thundering
herd of probes against the pool that is already struggling.


#### T019 delivery notes

The container holds the application's singletons: settings, engine, session factory, Redis client, and health service. It does not hold repository instances because they are bound to a session. Instead, it provides factory methods to create repositories given a session.

Construction is lazy: the engine, session factory, and Redis client are built on first access. This allows the container to be instantiated without connecting to external services, making it testable on a bare checkout.

The container registers the four health checks that are available today (PostgreSQL, Redis, Ollama, filesystem) using lambdas that capture the container's dependencies. The agent_runtime and event_bus checks are omitted and will be registered by their respective subsystems when implemented (T031, T044).

The container provides async context managers for session scopes: `session_scope` for transactional writes and `read_session_scope` for read-only operations that always roll back.

The container also includes startup and shutdown lifespan methods that ping the database and Redis, and dispose of resources.


#### T024 delivery notes â€” Phase 1 test suite

The listed areas were already covered by unit tests, so the work was to find the
gaps and to test the one thing no unit file could: the application *as wired*.

`tests/unit/test_api_smoke.py` (13 tests) is the only test that builds the real
app through `create_app()` and walks the whole route table. It found four real
problems, three of which no existing test could see.

**1. Two error shapes on one API.** A 404 from an unmatched path and a 405 from
a wrong method are raised by Starlette's router, not by ULTRON code, so they
never became an `UltronError` and bypassed the handler entirely. They answered
with FastAPI's bare `{"detail": "Not Found"}` while every other failure answered
`{"error": {...}}`. Every client would have needed a special case. Fixed with a
`StarletteHTTPException` handler plus a `_HTTP_ERROR_CODES` table, and a new
`ErrorCode.METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED` backed by `MethodNotAllowedError` (405 is
permanent, not retryable â€” repeating the identical request cannot help).

**2. `/health` and `/ready` were declared twice.** `create_app` mounted the
health router *and* defined both endpoints inline, after the mount. The inline
copies lost the match and were unreachable, while still reading as the real
handlers â€” a maintenance trap pointing at dead code. The duplicates are removed;
the router versions are authoritative. The OpenAPI schema **cannot** catch this:
duplicate path/method pairs collapse into one entry. The smoke test detects it
by asserting on the *shape of the live response* instead.

**3. `METRICS_ENABLED` defaulted to `true` in three places** (`Settings`,
`ObservabilitySettings`, `.env.example`) while the spec, `todo.md` C10 and the
T022 notes all said metrics are off unless enabled. The route tests passed the
flag in explicitly, so nothing ever exercised the shipped default â€” the tests
were green and the endpoint was open. Aligned the code to the documented intent
(`false`), and added `test_metrics_default_to_disabled` to assert the *default*
rather than a fixture-forced value.

**4. A latent test bug.** `settings_warned` in `test_health_routes.py` scraped
`/metrics` while relying on the default being `true`. Changing the default broke
it, which is the correct outcome â€” it had been asserting against a default
rather than the behaviour it meant to test.

Two things this file deliberately does not do: it does not enter the
`TestClient` context, so the container lifespan never runs and no database or
socket is opened; and it does not touch `/ready`, because readiness runs the
health engine, which takes seconds to fail against a real PostgreSQL and Redis.
`/health` is dependency-free and answers immediately.

**Closed with T023:** the event-stream connect check now lives in this file
(`test_event_stream_refuses_an_anonymous_caller`, plus `/events` in the
mounted-surface assertion) and runs against the real `create_app()` app. The
happy path cannot travel over `TestClient` â€” starlette 1.7 buffers an endless
stream to completion â€” so it is covered by the T023 route tests via direct
drive instead.

#### T020 delivery notes

The application factory creates a FastAPI instance with:
- The dependency injection container wired into a `lifespan` context manager
  (startup pings DB+Redis, shutdown disposes resources)
- Exception handling driven by each error's own `http_status` and `code`, so the
  wire contract lives in exactly one place (`app/core/errors.py`)
- Middleware configured from settings, not hardcoded: CORS origins and allowed
  hosts both come from configuration and both default to *closed*
- Health endpoints: `/health` (liveness) and `/ready` (readiness, answered by
  `HealthService` and 503 when a required check fails). Both are served by the
  health router mounted in `_include_routers`; T024 removed the duplicate inline
  definitions that used to sit alongside it in `create_app`
- Router mounting framework ready for T022â€“T024 (auth, connection manager, WS routes) and T036â€“T039 (agents, tasks, memories, conversations)

The application is importable without external services: `from app.main import create_app` works on a bare checkout, enabling testing and Docker/CI usage without running PostgreSQL or Redis.

#### T020 security hardening (spec Â§30, Â§31)

A security audit against Â§1, Â§15â€“17, Â§29â€“31 and Â§40 found the factory itself
was the weakest part of the code written so far. Six defects were real, not
hypothetical, and four of them were in this task's own output:

| Defect | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| CORS | `allow_origins=["*"]` with `allow_credentials=True` â€” the combination browsers reject, so it granted nothing while reading as permissive | Reads `CORS_ORIGINS`; empty means *disabled*; credentials only with explicit origins; mixing `*` with explicit origins is a startup `ConfigError` |
| TrustedHost | `allowed_hosts=["*"]`, with no setting to change it | Reads the new `ALLOWED_HOSTS`; middleware is only installed when configured |
| Error codes | `exc.error_code` â€” an attribute that does not exist, so every response was labelled `INTERNAL_ERROR` | `exc.code.value` |
| Error statuses | A hand-written `isinstance` ladder that had drifted from `errors.py`: permission denials and `CommandNotAllowedError` returned **500** instead of 403, `SerializationError` returned 400 instead of 500, and `DatabaseError` had no branch at all | `exc.http_status`, asserted against the contract by a parametrized test |
| Validation errors | `exc.errors()` straight into `JSONResponse`: `ctx` holds exception objects, so a client mistake became a 500 â€” and `input` would have reflected an attempted password back from `/auth` | Reduced to `loc`/`msg`/`type`, and JSON-encoded defensively |
| Logging | `configure_logging()` was never called, so the redaction layer was inert and `logging.lastResort` printed raw records to stderr | Called first in `create_app`, before anything logs |
| `/ready` | Hardcoded `{"status": "ready"}` â€” reported health the process did not have | Delegates to `HealthService`; 503 when a required check fails |
| `/docs` | Served in every environment, cataloguing the whole route surface | Disabled when `ENVIRONMENT=production` |

Two further defects were found in T019 code by the new tests:

- `container.py` referenced `settings.workspace`; the property is `workspaces`.
  The filesystem health check would have raised `AttributeError` at runtime, so
  the workspace boundary was never actually being probed.
- `Container.read_session_scope` had lost its `@asynccontextmanager` decorator,
  so `async with container.read_session_scope()` failed at runtime.

`ALLOWED_HOSTS` was added to `.env.example`, and `startup_warnings()` now also
warns in production about a `0.0.0.0` bind and an empty `CORS_ORIGINS`. That
function existed and was never called, so a deployment could run for weeks with
a placeholder admin password and nothing would say so.

Ruff now selects the `S` (flake8-bandit) rules, which is the family that flags
`allow_origins=["*"]` combined with `allow_credentials=True` â€” the pair of
checks that should have caught all of this the first time. Test files are
exempt from the hardcoded-secret rules because their fixtures exist to prove
redaction works.

`tests/unit/test_main.py` adds 40 tests. The three security-critical
behaviours were mutation-checked: restoring wildcard CORS fails 4 of them,
reintroducing the `error_code` typo fails 5, and making `/ready` always answer
200 fails 1.

Still missing, and tracked as later tasks rather than fixed here: permission
enforcement (T033), rate limiting (T035) and the tool execution pipeline
(T036). Event-stream authentication has since closed with T023 (`fetch()` +
`Authorization` header; `todo.md` Â§G2).

#### Security audit: remaining gaps against Â§15/Â§16/Â§17/Â§30/Â§31

The same audit confirmed that the *design* for these requirements is already in
place; what is missing is the code that uses it. Nothing below is a surprise to
the spec â€” it is a list of schemas, settings and error types with no caller.

| Requirement | Ready | Missing | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| Secure password handling | `users.password_hash` (Argon2id), `SecuritySettings` cost params | No hashing, no verification; `argon2-cffi` never imported | T022 |
| Session / token architecture | `sessions` with `token_hash`, `refresh_token_hash`, rotation chain, reuse detection | No issuance or verification; `PyJWT` never imported | T022 |
| API keys for machine clients | `users.is_service_account`, `api_key_hash` | No issue/revoke path | T022 |
| Device authentication | `devices.auth_token_hash`, `ESP32_REQUIRE_AUTH` | Never read or checked | T024 |
| Role / permission checks | `PermissionLevel` L0â€“L5 with `allows()` | Nothing consumes it; no role enum | T033 |
| Audit log for sensitive operations | `AuditLogEntry`, `AuditOutcome`, `AuditLogRepository` | No row is ever written | T033 |
| Rate limiting | `rate_limit_enabled`, `rate_limit_requests` | No middleware | T035 |
| Schema validation (pipeline stage 1) | â€” | â€” | T035/T036 |
| SSRF guard | `is_disallowed_host`, fails closed on unresolvable names and metadata IPs | Only stubbed; no caller | T036 |
| Shell allow-list | `TerminalSettings.allowed_commands=[]` denies everything | No matcher | T036 |
| Filesystem boundary | `WorkspaceSettings.allowed_paths()` | Unenforced outside the health check | T036 |
| `/metrics` | `prometheus-client`, `metrics_path` | Never mounted | T022 |

Two settings are also unused scaffolding and are worth wiring before Phase 1 is

#### T023 delivery notes â€” SSE event stream

`277aac0` wrote the endpoint and its tests but never ran a gate; this entry
closes that debt and records what the gate found.

**What exists.** `app/api/routes/events.py`: `GET /events`, authenticated by
the standard `Authorization: Bearer` dependency, subscribes to the real event
bus and sends named server-sent events until the client disconnects. Topic
selection validates against the known vocabulary (422 naming what is allowed);
`Last-Event-ID` / `lastEventId` resume a dropped stream from the event store;
`Retry:` hints are emitted while the backoff itself lives client-side
(`fetch()`, never `EventSource`, so the token never enters a URL or proxy log
â€” `todo.md` Â§G2). 503 on subscription-capacity exhaustion, heartbeats keep
proxies from idling the connection, and every exit path (client disconnect,
capacity refusal, bus error) unsubscribes so nothing leaks.

**The gate found three structural problems, none visible before it:**

1. **`TestClient` cannot stream.** starlette 1.7's transport runs the app to
   completion and buffers the whole response, so a *successful* endless SSE
   request hangs forever â€” `client.stream` included, because headers do not
   return until the app finishes. The 11 open-stream tests now drive
   `stream_events` directly through a typed `open_stream()` helper;
   `TestClient` remains correct for the finite paths (401/422/503).
2. **Ambient environment leaked into the test suite.** This machine's shell
   exports `ENABLE_PGVECTOR=false` (user scope), so model import picked `JSONB`
   for `memories.embedding` while `clean_settings` fixtures claimed the default
   `True`. `tests/conftest.py` now scrubs every settings env var at import
   time, before models load.
3. **mypy had never seen the test tree.** `explicit_package_bases` plus
   `mypy_path` resolved the `conftest` vs `tests.conftest` duplicate-module
   clash, exposing 26 real typing errors across 5 test files (untyped route
   locals, `Response | Awaitable[Response]`, dead `type: ignore`s).

**Evidence:** ruff check + format + mypy clean over 108 files;
`scripts/test.ps1` = 1021 passed (smoke 14); `test_event_stream_routes.py`
28/28 in 2s.

## Capability extensions (spec Â§59, added after T020)

Spec Â§59 is additive: it extends sections 1-58 rather than replacing them, and
Â§59.1 maps every requested capability to the section it extends so nothing is
built twice. Tasks are numbered T220+ to avoid colliding with the original
sequence.

**These are not next.** The build continues through Phase 1 (T022+) in order;
this block records the work so it is not rediscovered later. Nothing here may be
started before its dependency in Â§59.28.

### Phase 3 additions â€” model system (Â§59.7-Â§59.9)

- [x] **T220** Model capability registry (Â§59.8) â€” per-model record: id, provider, context length, tool/reasoning/coding/vision support, resource class, discovered availability, `free`/`paid`. Dependency: T060. Blocks T221, T223.
- [x] **T221** `app/models/openrouter.py` (Â§59.7) â€” OpenRouter adapter, **free models only**. Refuses a non-free configured model at settings load with `ConfigError`; optional key; 429 mapped to `ModelRateLimited`, not an outage. Dependency: T220.
- [x] **T222** Model capability probe â€” refresh `availability` from the provider so a delisted free model degrades instead of failing. Dependency: T221.
- [x] **T223** Fallback chain + failure taxonomy (Â§59.9) â€” per-capability chain, bounded retries with exponential backoff, failures classified into the existing typed errors, **context overflow is not retried**, each hop recorded in `model_usage` (T067). Dependency: T221.
- [x] **T224** Degradation is visible â€” a fallback to Ollama is surfaced to the user and to observability, never a silent weaker answer. Dependency: T223. Implemented: selection_reason threading to ModelResponse, ToolLoopResult, ChatResponse and SSE done frame; exposed via chat API. Tests passing. All pending Phase-3 model tasks complete.

### Phase 5 additions â€” tool runtime (Â§59.3-Â§59.6)

- [x] **T230** Git tools as structured tools (Â§59.3) â€” one tool per operation, three tiers: SAFE (status/diff/log/branch/show), CONTROLLED (create/switch branch, commit, pull, stash), EXPLICIT AUTHORIZATION (push, force-push, `reset --hard`, `clean -fd`, `branch -D`, rebase, `filter-branch`). Level declared on the tool so Â§16 enforces it; **no shell fallback**. Dependency: T036. Implementation verified: `app/tools/git/` family with SAFE/CONTROLLED/EXPLICIT tiers, permission levels declared, literal argv via GitRunner, sandbox path resolution, tests passed.
- [x] **T231** Filesystem layer (Â§59.4) â€” real-path resolution (symlinks followed, `..` collapsed) **before** the `WorkspaceSettings.allowed_paths()` test; read/write/list/stat/search/move/copy/delete; delete and out-of-workspace write at least CONTROLLED. Dependency: T036. Implementation verified: `FilesystemSandbox` resolves real paths before allow-list check; tools `filesystem.read/write/list/search/delete` present with correct permission levels; tests passed.
- [x] **T232** Server management tools (Â§59.5) â€” cpu/ram/disk/temperature/processes/services/logs/ports/network/uptime/docker status; one structured tool per operation; every tool time-bounded; restart/stop at EXPLICIT AUTHORIZATION; `deploy` acts on an approved definition only. Dependency: T036. Implementation verified: `app/tools/system/` tools (cpu/memory/disk/processes/network/gpu/services/ollama/docker) with READ_ONLY permission and timeouts; tests passed.
- [x] **T233** Tool registry completeness (Â§59.6) â€” assert every registered tool declares name, description, `input_schema`, `permission_level`, `execute`, `verify`, timeout, logging, error mapping, audit flag; categories `filesystem git terminal process network server docker browser search database http notifications voice audio display automation`. Dependency: T035. Implementation verified: `ToolRegistry` with `describe()` and category derivation; Tool ABC enforces all declarations at import time; Phase 5 tests cover completeness/uniqueness.
- [x] **T234** Audit an agent cannot bypass â€” a test proving a direct tool import (bypassing the Tool Router) fails the permission check. Dependency: T036. Implementation verified: Phase 5 tests include agent bypass verification; ToolExecutor enforces permission pipeline.

### Agent additions (Â§59.10-Â§59.12, Â§59.22)

- [x] **T240** Research citations (Â§59.10) â€” per-source extraction before synthesis; every claim bound to a collected source; unsupported claims omitted or marked; **model output is never a source**. Dependency: T036. Implementation exists in `app/agents/research/` and tests. Marked complete.
- [x] **T241** Decision-support agent (Â§59.11) â€” options / criteria / documented facts / tradeoffs / unknowns / missing constraints; presents rather than silently decides; recommendations labelled with reasoning and uncertainty; asks for missing constraints instead of assuming. Dependency: T047 (routing). Needs no tools, so it can be built early. Implemented in `app/agents/decision_support/` with `DecisionSupportAgent`, `DecisionReport`, `Option`, `Criterion`, `Tradeoff`, `Unknown`, `MissingConstraint`, `Alternative`. Agent declares `agent_type = "decision.support"`, capability `"reasoning"`, no tools required. Structured output parsed via regex from model response.
- [x] **T242** Health-information agent (Â§59.12) â€” informational only. **Boundaries enforced in the agent/tool layer, not by prompt**: no self-presentation as a doctor, no diagnosis with certainty, no dangerous treatment instructions, no autonomous high-stakes decisions. Informational framing only; routes to professional care; **not related to `HealthService`**. Dependency: T033. â€” **done.** Written: `server/app/agents/health_info/__init__.py`, `agent.py`, `report.py`. Structural safety boundaries (not prompt-only): pre-model emergency/crisis detection routes immediately ahead of any model call; post-model regex validation refuses diagnosis/treatment/prescription/prognosis output; mandatory disclaimer appended to every non-emergency response; professional direction injected when question mentions severe/persistent symptoms or when output is structurally refused. Outputs structured `HealthReport` with `frames` (categorized info blocks), `refused` (category+reason pairs), `professional_direction`, and `uncertainty_summary`. Uses `capability="reasoning"` default per Â§59.8. Tests: `tests/unit/test_health_info_agent.py` (17 tests â€” basic query, emergency detection x5 keywords, diagnosis/treatment/prescription/prognosis structural refusal, professional direction on severe/refused, mandatory disclaimer, missing question error, progress events, task ID propagation, uncertainty summary, frame categories, self-harm detection, boundary overrides prompt, unconfigured provider refusal). 17 passed.

### Voice additions (Â§59.16-Â§59.20)

- [x] **T250** Local STT on the stated hardware (Â§59.17) â€” smallest viable `faster-whisper`, **no GPU assumption**, chunked/streaming where practical, partial vs final results, VAD and silence detection. Dependency: Phase 9 STT.
  - Evidence: `app/voice/stt/faster_whisper.py` `FasterWhisperSTT` declares `sample_rate=16000` (whisper-native, so callers resample once), loads the model lazily on first use, and streams via `transcribe_stream`, emitting `TranscriptChunk(is_final=False)` partials and an `is_final=True` close. VAD is not re-derived: the engine consumes the caller's `AudioChunk.is_speech` (Â§59.19 requires one shared VAD), and `UtteranceBoundary` (T251) owns silence detection. `_decode_audio` converts the transport's int16 PCM to the float32 array whisper expects, importing `numpy` lazily and raising `DependencyUnavailableError("stt", â€¦)` when it is absent â€” no GPU package is imported, and `stt/availability.py` probes the install honestly. The cloud adapter `GroqWhisperSTT` runs the same interface when a key is present.
- [x] **T251** **Utterance-boundary rule** (Â§59.17) â€” a pause must not cause the preceding audio to be retransmitted as a new utterance; explicit boundaries + overlap policy, deduplicated, testable in isolation. Fixture: a mid-sentence pause yields one utterance with no duplicated text. Dependency: T250. **Design this before the STT adapter, not after.**
  - Evidence: the policy is separate, pure and synchronous in `app/voice/stt/utterance.py` (D015). `UtteranceBoundary` ends a turn only after a **run** of silent chunks reaches `silence_threshold`, so a breath or mid-sentence pause keeps one utterance open and the next speech is appended rather than retransmitted. `OverlapDeduper` strips the duplicated head of a sliding window (longest suffix/prefix match, bounded by `max_overlap`) so a re-read tail is not spoken twice. Both are asserted directly in `tests/unit/test_voice_stt.py` with no whisper install, and `AudioChunk.end_of_utterance` lets a client-supplied boundary (browser VAD / push-to-talk) close the turn explicitly when the client alone knows the user stopped.
- [x] **T252** Local TTS interface + Piper (Â§59.18) â€” engine behind an interface, Piper default, priority order latency > RAM > CPU > naturalness > offline > chunked; sentence-level synthesis; voice configurable. Dependency: Phase 9 TTS.
  - Evidence: `app/voice/tts/base.py` `TTSEngine` is the interface (with `content_type` so the transport knows the downlink encoding). `app/voice/tts/piper.py` `PiperTTS` is the local default; `app/voice/tts/edge_tts.py` `EdgeTTSTTS` is a second local engine and `app/voice/tts/elevenlabs.py` a cloud one, all behind the same ABC and selected by config. `app/voice/tts/segments.py` `split_sentences` enables sentence-level synthesis so speaking starts on the first sentence. Voice, rate and output format are configurable through `VoiceSettings` (`tts_voice`, `tts_sample_rate`, `elevenlabs_output_format`). A missing Piper binary degrades to `available=False` rather than failing at boot (Â§59.18, Â§64.11).
- [x] **T253** Barge-in and cancellation (Â§59.19) â€” stop TTS immediately on user speech, cancel the in-flight response, discard queued synthesis; **a cancelled synthesis must actually stop filling its buffer**; interrupted partial turn marked, not silently dropped. Dependency: T250, T252.
  - Evidence: `app/voice/manager.py` checks `session.interrupt` **before delivering each synthesized chunk**, so a cancelled synthesis stops filling its buffer at the next chunk rather than draining a queue. `interrupt()` sets the flag and cancels the pipeline task; `barge_in()` sets only the flag so a long-lived WebSocket session keeps listening after the user talks over ULTRON. An interrupted assistant turn is recorded as `VoiceTurn(..., interrupted=True)` â€” marked, never dropped â€” and `TTS_COMPLETED` carries `interrupted`. `tests/unit/test_voice_manager.py` and `tests/unit/test_voice_routes.py` assert the truncation, the flag, and the marked turn.
- [skip ] **T254** LiveKit self-hosted transport (Â§59.16) â€” room/session orchestration for mic in and audio out; budgeted in the Â§48 RAM plan; **a room token is not a ULTRON credential** and a voice session still resolves to an authenticated principal before any agent runs. Dependency: T253. - `REVISED` - cloud LiveKit is the default (spec 59.16). Self-hosting stays supported and stays deferred; no LiveKit server runs on the 4 GB VM or the 8 GB laptop.
- [ skip] **T255** Offline voice path (Â§59.20) â€” local STT small whisper with no transport at all; capabilities the local model lacks are reported unavailable rather than answered more weakly. Dependency: T254. - `FUTURE` - fully offline voice remains supported but is explicitly out of scope until hardware allows it.

**Voice delivery decisions (T250-T253).** T250-T253 were delivered over an **in-app WebSocket transport** (`WS /voice/ws`, D040) rather than LiveKit: a browser cannot be a LiveKit SFU client without a running room server, none is allowed on this hardware (Â§59.25), and the Â§59.19 requirements (partial transcripts, one shared VAD, barge-in, streaming playback) are carried by the manager and the socket without one. T254 (LiveKit) remains a deferred option behind the same `VoiceManager` seam, exactly as T255's fully-offline path does.

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D040 | Voice reaches clients through an in-app WebSocket (`GET /voice/engines` + `WS /voice/ws`), not a LiveKit room server; engines are **cloud-first** (`groq` STT, `elevenlabs` TTS) with local (`faster-whisper`, `piper`, `edge-tts`) fallbacks, and turn-taking uses a client-side VAD that sends an explicit `utterance_end` frame while raw PCM streams up | No LiveKit server is permitted on the 4 GB target or the 8 GB laptop (Â§59.25), and a room token is not an ULTRON credential (Â§59.16); the `VoiceManager` seam keeps T254/T255 as drop-in transports. Cloud-first matches Â§59.17/Â§59.18 (local engines are optional, never assumed), and a browser already has a reliable VAD, so the boundary is decided where the audio is captured rather than re-derived server-side |

### Extension decisions

| # | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D011 | OpenRouter is integrated free-models-only, and a non-free configured model is a startup `ConfigError` | The design must not depend on paid inference. Failing at startup is the only outcome that gets noticed, and free-tier 429s are handled as a routing condition (Â§59.9) rather than an outage |
| D012 | Extension sections append to `server_arc.md` as Â§59 and map to the existing sections | Avoids a second source of truth. Â§59.1 records, per capability, whether it is new or an extension, so a reader can tell real work from restatement |
| D013 | Extension tasks are T220+, appended as a separate block rather than renumbered into Phases 3/5/9 | The existing IDs are referenced from `tasks.md`, the Decision log and the phase status log. Renumbering would break those references for no benefit |
| D014 | Extensions are recorded but **not scheduled ahead of Phase 1** | Â§53 and Â§57 require finishing the current phase in order. T025-T027 are still blocked on Docker, but skipping ahead would abandon the phase gate rather than work around it |
| D015 | Utterance-boundary STT requirement is specified in Â§59.17, not left to the adapter | It is a named defect in the current voice behaviour ("every pause retranscribes the previous sentence as a new utterance"). A requirement stated at implementation time reads as a nicety; stated now it is testable |
| D016 | Health-information boundaries are enforced in the agent/tool layer, not by prompt | A prompt is a preference that a capable model can be talked out of. A refusal at the layer holds regardless of what was asked, which is the only acceptable standard for this category |

#### T021 delivery notes (in progress â€” not yet committed)

Authentication is the last untouched piece of Â§30, and the gap table above says
the *design* was already in place with no caller. This closes four of those rows.

**Delivered so far**

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `app/security/passwords.py` | Argon2id only; verify, `needs_rehash`, dummy-verify equaliser |
| `app/security/tokens.py` | Opaque 256-bit tokens, SHA-256 digests, bearer extraction, prefixes |
| `app/security/audit.py` | `AuditLogger` with `allowed`/`denied`/`confirm_required` |
| `app/security/authentication.py` | login, token auth, refresh+rotation, logout, API keys, device auth, superuser guard |
| `app/api/dependencies.py` | container access, per-request session, authenticator, `require_principal`, `require_superuser`, correlation id |
| `app/api/routes/auth.py` | `POST /auth/login`, `/refresh`, `/logout`, `GET /auth/me`, `POST /auth/password` |
| `app/main.py` | `RequestContextMiddleware` â€” correlation id + one access log line per request |

**Decisions worth recording**

- **Opaque tokens, not JWT.** The schema already models sessions with
  `token_hash`, `refresh_token_hash`, a rotation chain and revocation, which is a
  revocable-session design. A JWT would have to be reconciled against those
  rows to be revocable at all, at which point it has bought nothing. `PyJWT`
  stays unused. Tokens are 256 bits of `secrets` output; digests are SHA-256 so
  the indexed `token_hash` lookup works.
- **SHA-256 for tokens and API keys, Argon2id only for passwords.** An API key is
  256 bits of random, so there is no dictionary to search and a slow KDF would
  only add latency while making the indexed `api_key_hash` unusable. Argon2 is
  reserved for the one credential that is low-entropy and human-chosen.
- **One transaction per request.** `get_db_session` opens a `session_scope` for
  the whole request and the repositories and audit logger are built from that one
  session. Repositories never commit (Â§15/T013), so a login that creates a
  session row and an audit row lands together or not at all â€” and a route cannot
  commit while its audit entry rolls back.
- **`get_container` returns a `Protocol`, not `object`.** `object` plus
  `type: ignore` on every attribute access was hiding the wiring. The protocol is
  structurally checked against the real `Container` at type-check time, so
  changing the container shape now fails `mypy` instead of failing on the first
  request that touches the changed member.
- **The container is accessed via a protocol, not imported**, so the dependency
  module has no import-time dependency on the wiring graph and tests can supply
  a stub.
- **A refusal that must change state uses its own transaction.** Denials end in a
  raise, and the raise rolls the request transaction back. Audit rows therefore
  go through `DurableAuditSink`, and reuse-detection's family revocation through
  `DurableFamilyRevoker`. The rule is not "audit durably" but "anything whose
  loss would leave a security decision unenforced writes durably" â€” an audit row
  that vanishes is a blind spot, but a revoked session that comes back is a
  compromise.

**Defects the tests caught in this task's own code**

These are recorded because each was a real bug, not a test artefact, and each is
the kind that unit tests written alongside the code tend to assume away.

| Defect | Why it mattered |
|---|---|
| `AuditLogger` caught only `DatabaseError` | Its contract says it never raises unless `strict`. A failure inside the append itself escaped and turned an audit write into an outage. Caught by a test using a non-`DatabaseError` failure |
| `dummy_verify` cached one hash globally | Verified against whatever Argon2 parameters were configured *now*, so raising the cost at runtime reopened the timing gap it exists to close. Now keyed by the parameter tuple |
| Refresh reuse detection was unreachable, and had a false positive | The revoked check was folded into `is_refreshable`, so a replayed token looked like an ordinary dead token â€” the exact case that must not be silent. The replacement test on `rotated_from` was worse: it is set on the *new* session, so it would have flagged the **second legitimate refresh** as reuse and revoked the family. Reuse now rests on the structural fact that `SessionRepository.get_by_refresh_token_hash`'s docstring already specifies: a consumed token resolves to an already-revoked session |
| `session.revoke()` ignored the injected clock | Three call sites omitted `now=`, so revocation timestamps came from `datetime.now()` while everything else used the injected clock. A clock that is honoured everywhere except one path is worse than none, because tests cannot observe it |
| `PermissionDeniedError(msg, details=...)` raised `TypeError` | The class builds its own `details`, so passing another one is a duplicate keyword. Found by the superuser-guard tests |
| **Reuse detection revoked the session family in the transaction it then rolled back** | The one path where a *refusal* must still change durable state. It wrote the denial, revoked the family, then raised `AuthError` â€” and `session_scope` rolls back that raise, so the revocation was discarded while the audit row survived. Net effect: an alert with no effect, and the attacker kept a usable rotated-out session. Fixed with a `DurableFamilyRevoker` that revokes on its own connection (`RevokeFamily` protocol), the same treatment the audit denial already had |
| **Test isolation did not cover import-time settings reads** | `memories.embedding`'s column type is chosen while the model module is *imported*, i.e. during collection, which happens before any `autouse` fixture runs. The developer's real `.env` therefore decided the schema while every test body observed default settings. Green on a machine with no `.env`, wrong on a machine with one. `ULTRON_ENV_FILE` is now installed at conftest *import* time, which is early enough for collection |

**Closed on T021**

- Route-level tests (`/auth/*` over `TestClient`, correlation-id echo and
  sanitisation, anonymous-mode switch): **35 tests, passing.**
- The full gate: **ruff clean, mypy clean (84 source files), 912 tests passing.**
- Verified against a **live PostgreSQL 17** instance with
  `ENABLE_PGVECTOR=false` (17 tables migrated, `memories.embedding` is `jsonb`):
  login, token authentication, refresh rotation and replay refusal all behave,
  and the audit trail carries `auth.login` / `auth.token_refresh` /
  `auth.token_reuse_detected`. This is a scratch schema for tests and migrations;
  ULTRON is never run on this machine (Â§61).
- Committed and pushed.

**Still open on T021**

- ~~`security.require_auth` is not consulted by `require_principal`~~ â€”
  **withdrawn, it was wrong.** There is no `security.require_auth`. The only
  `require_auth` in settings is `devices.esp32_require_auth`
  (`ESP32_REQUIRE_AUTH`, `settings.py:459`), a device-transport setting that has
  nothing to do with HTTP auth. `require_principal` branches on
  `security.allow_anonymous` (default `False`), which is the correct and only
  guard, so there was no bypass to close. Adding a `security.require_auth` now
  would be inventing a second switch for a decision one flag already makes.
- The genuine related gap, tracked under T022: `ALLOW_ANONYMOUS` only produces a
    `startup_warnings` entry, and `.env.example` says it "must be false in
    production" without anything enforcing it. **Closed by T022** â€” not by
    failing the boot, but by making the warning visible: `/health` now reports
    `status: degraded` and lists the warnings, so a misconfigured production
    deploy is diagnosable instead of silently healthy. That is what the settings
    docstring always intended by choosing warnings over boot failures.
- Logout's docstring claims a malformed `Authorization` header is ignored, but
  only a *missing* header is; a non-empty malformed value still raises from
  `tokens.extract_bearer`. Low severity, but the doc and the behaviour should
  agree.
